Pseudo:
```
def evaluate_pos_neg(h,n):
  pos = np.empty(n); neg = np.empty(n)
  for l in range(n):
    pos[l]=np.dot(h[:n-l], 1-h[l:])
    neg[l]=np.dot(h[l:], 1-h[:n-l]) if l>0 else pos[0]
  return pos, neg
```

```
def compute_grad_soft(h, pos, neg, beta, n):
  vpos=pos*dx; vneg=neg*dx
  vmax=max(max(vpos), max(vneg))
  ep = np.exp(beta*(vpos-vmax))
  en = np.exp(beta*(vneg[1:]-vmax)) # lag0 only pos
  qpos = ep / (ep.sum()+en.sum())
  qneg = np.zeros(n); qneg[1:] = en / total
  # F = vmax + np.log(total)/beta
  # linear L
  L = np.zeros(n)
  # pos contribution: for j, sum_{l=0}^{n-1-j} qpos[l]
  # qpos cumulative from end
  cum = np.cumsum(qpos[::-1])[::-1] # cum[t]=sum qpos[t:]
  L += cum[n-1:]? For j, sum l=0 to n-1-j = qpos[:n-j] sum.
```

```
Need maintain feasible h exactly. Projection function:
```
def project_box_sum(x, target):
  # minimize ||x-y|| with 0<=y<=1, sum=y=target
  # bisection on tau: y = clip(x - tau, 0,1)
  lo, hi = x.min()-1? Actually tau can be [x.min()-1, x.max()]. Solve sum clip(x - tau) = target.
  for iter in 50:
    mid=(lo+hi)/2; y=np.clip(x-mid,0,1); s=y.sum(); if s>target: lo=mid else hi=mid
  return clip(x-(lo+hi)/2)
```
Since target integer n/2. Need `np.clip(0,1)`. 60 iterations maybe. For speed, can use specialized? 400*60*iter ~ 24k operations, fine.

Line search:
- Starting h feasible, scale step. Gradient g (unconstrained). Use projected step: y = project(h - alpha*g). Since g may have constant component; projection handles. We can also subtract mean of g to reduce step? Because sum constraint, constant gradient doesn't matter? Soft max gradient may have constant components, projection will adjust.
- Armijo: F_new < F_cur + c*alpha*dot(g, y-h) (directional derivative projected?) Since nonconvex, use simple backtracking: if F_new < F_cur - reduction or F_new < F_cur accept if within max. We can do generic: start al = 1e-3 or BB. Try decreasing, accept if F_new <= F_cur + 1e-12*abs(F_cur) (allow slight increase) maybe? For nonconvex, accept if F_new < F_cur or alpha small. Stagnation risk.
- BB step: hull from last gradient/point: α = ||Δh||^2 / (||Δh||^2 + grad dot Δh)? Standard. Or use fixed decreasing with noise.

Projected gradient may be slow to converge to very high beta. But can use SLSQP as main.

SLSQP with gradient:
```
def obj_grad(x, beta):
  pos,neg=compute_pos_neg(x)
  F, qpos, qneg = soft_info(pos,neg,beta)
  grad = compute_grad(qpos,qneg)
  return F, grad
res = minimize(lambda x: obj_grad(x)[0], x0, jac=lambda x: obj_grad(x)[1], method='SLSQP', bounds, constraints={'type':'eq','fun':sum_target}, options={'maxiter':500,'ftol':1e-12})
```

```
def compute_pos_neg(h,n):
    pos = np.empty(n)
    neg = np.empty(n)
    # h squares sum for l=0
    pos[0] = np.dot(h, h)? c0= sum h - sum h^2
    pos[0] = np.sum(h) - np.dot(h,h)
    neg[0] = pos[0]
    for l in range(1,n):
        a = h[:n-l]
        b = h[l:]
        pos[l] = a.sum() - np.dot(a,b)
        neg[l] = b.sum() - np.dot(b,a)
    return pos, neg
```
Since sum(h)=target, a.sum() not constant. For feasible h sum target but substeps during SLSQP may not exact? If using SLSQP constraints, h feasible, but line search projected feasible. Use dot. Could precompute? We can compute `h.sum()` each? a.sum() cheap. But for many l, sum slices repeated. We can precompute prefix sums H = np.cumsum(h) to compute a.sum() = H[n-l-1] (if l>0), b.sum()=target-H[l-1]. This speeds. Do that:
```
H = np.concatenate(([0], np.cumsum(h)))
for l in range(1,n):
  pos[l] = H[n-l] - np.dot(h[:n-l], h[l:])
  neg[l] = H[n]-H[l] - np.dot(h[l:], h[:n-l])
```
But dot symmetric: np.dot(a,b)=np.dot(b,a), so same product p. pos = sum a - p, neg = sum b - p. Good. Use `np.dot(h[:n-l], h[l:])`.
For l=0, product dot(h,h), sum target.
This is O(n^2) vector. Could use FFT for values if needed. n=400 fine.

Could compute all via matrix? no.

`soft_weights(pos,neg,beta,n)`:
```
def soft_info(pos,neg,beta,n):
  dx=2.0/n
  vpos=pos*dx
  vmax=vpos[0]
  if neg.size>1: vmax=max(vmax, neg[1:].max())
  ep=np.exp(beta*(vpos-vmax))
  en=np.empty(n); en[0]=0
  if n>1: en[1:]=np.exp(beta*(neg[1:]-vmax))
  total=ep.sum()+en.sum()
  qpos=ep/total; qneg=en/total
  F = vmax + np.log(total)/beta
  return F, qpos, qneg
```
But if n=1 impossible sum=0.5. okay.
Need ensure qneg[0]=0; qpos includes lag0.
Potential underflow if beta large and v-vmax very negative; exp 0 okay. If beta too large, gradient only max lags; fine.
`F` could be slightly > max; best bound final should use hard max not soft.
```

```
Implement `gradient_for_weights(h,qpos,qneg,n)`:
```
def grad_for_weights(h,qpos,qneg,n):
  dx=2.0/n
  # L
  L = np.cumsum(qpos)[::-1] + np.cumsum(qneg)
  K = np.zeros(n)
  K[0] = 2*qpos[0]
  if n>1: K[1:] = qpos[1:] + qneg[1:]
  kernel = np.zeros(n)
  # d=0
  kernel += K[0]*h
  for d in range(1,n):
     coeff=K[d]
     if coeff == 0: continue
     kernel[:-d] += coeff * h[d:]
     kernel[d:] += coeff * h[:-d]
  grad = dx * (L - kernel)
  return grad
```
This computes kernel_term. 399 loops. For q weights sparse? Could skip zero. Good.

But for large beta, q mostly top few lags, K has few nonzero. We can optimize by looping only nonzero distances: `nz = np.nonzero(K[1:])[0]+1`. This helps. We can also cap weights. Good.
```
for d in nonzero:
  coeff = K[d]
  ...
```
For beta small all nonzero.
```

```
`projected_gradient_refine(h, beta, time_limit)`:
Could be used for polish. Implement with BB and backtrack.
```
def grad_descent_refine(h,beta,deadline, iters=20000):
  target = n/2
  h = project_feasible(h,n)
  F,_,_ = calc
  h_prev = h.copy(); F_prev=F
  g = grad
  alpha=0.05
  for it in range(iters):
    # maybe recompute g
    y = project_feasible(h - alpha*g, n)
    # line search: try alphas [alpha, alpha*0.5,...] accept if F_y < F - 1e-12 or F_y < F + ...
    # Use directional derivative maybe
    dy = y-h
    step = 0
    accepted=False
    for trial in range(10):
       al=alpha
       y=project_feasible(h - al*g)
       Fy=calc(y)[0]
       if Fy <= F + 1e-14*max(1,abs(F)) or Fy < F - 1e-12:
           accept
       al *=0.5
    if accepted:
       # update H-BB
       delta = y - h
       gnew = grad(y)
       denom = np.dot(delta,gnew-g)
       if denom>1e-12:
          alpha = 0.5 * (np.dot(delta, gnew-g)/np.dot(gnew-g,gnew-g))? No.
       h=y; F=Fy; g=gnew
    else:
       if alpha < 1e-6: # accept small maybe stochastic
       break
  return h,F
```
But gradient recompute for each line search expensive. Simpler accept first alpha, recompute. BB: Use gradient difference. Standard BB: α = (Δx^T Δx)/(Δx^T Δg) or (Δx^T Δg)/(Δg^T Δg). We can keep stable.
Given SLSQP main, gradient descent maybe optional.

**Binary/bit local search**

Could improve discrete objective directly. Since final bound evaluated discretely, binary or near-binary local search may find good discrete. We can implement fast evaluate using np.correlate? For binary, C integer multiples. Could use local search with delta via direct update of full correlation vector. We can maintain corr_full = np.correlate(h,1-h,'full') (length M). For swap i zero to one, j one to zero, compute delta vector length M in O(n) with vectorized formulas, update corr, accept if max improves. Need delta exact. Let's derive exact delta in terms of full correlation array mapping. Need map full index to signed lag. To use delta formulas, choose full index `p = n-1 + s` for signed lag s (pos if s>0? where c_s = ∑ h_i(1-h_{i+s})). Is np.correlate full mapping? For max we can maintain our own corr_s array of length 2n-1 with index n-1+s matching pos/neg. We don't need match np.correlate if we evaluate final with our c5_s (max). But harness uses np; max same. For local search, maintain c_s exactly for signed lags:
`c_s = sum_i h_i(1-h_{i+s})` over valid.
For s=0: sum h_i(1-h_i).
For s>0: pos[s]. For s<0: neg[|s|]? If s=-l: ∑_{i=l}^{n-1} h_i(1-h_{i-l}) = neg[l]. So full_s index n-1+s: s positive pos, negative neg.
Then max_s = max c_s.
Swap delta exact for binary h (old i=0,j=1). Let's derive for c_s signed:
Each ordered pair (a,b) contributes to c_{b-a} term h_a(1-h_b). Swap changes h_i=0→1, h_j=1→0. For all pairs (a,b) involving i or j. We can compute delta by considering:
- Pairs (i,q) for q≠i: lag q-i, term old 0, new 1-h_q. delta = 1 - h_q.
- Pairs (q,i) for q≠i: lag i-q, term old h_q(1-0)=h_q, new h_q(1-1)=0. delta = -h_q.
- Pairs (j,q): lag q-j, term old (1-h_q), new 0. delta = -(1-h_q).
- Pairs (q,j): lag j-q, term old h_q(1-1)=0, new h_q. delta = h_q.
But for q=i in j pairs with old h_i=0, q=j in i pairs with old h_j=1. The sum of four for q between i and j? Let's include q=all except each. If we sum all these independently, for q=i in j pairs: pair (j,i) old 1 (since h_j=1,h_i=0), new 0 -> delta -1; pair (i,j) old 0,new 1 -> +1. Net 0? Wait pair (i,j) is included in Pairs (i,q) q=j: delta = 1 - h_j =0; Pairs (j,q) q=i: delta = -(1 - h_i) = -1. Pairs (q,i) q=j: delta = -h_j = -1; Pairs (q,j) q=i: delta = h_i =0. Total for lags? Let's track lag indices:
- (i,j): lag j-i delta 0
- (j,i): lag i-j delta -1
- (j,i) as Pairs (j,q) q=i also same? I double counted! Pairs (j,q) q=i is pair (j,i) with lag i-j. We already included? Actually pair (j,i) is first-index j second i: in Pairs (j,q) (q=i) delta -(1-h_i)=-1; and in Pairs (q,i) (q=j) delta -h_j=-1? That's wrong; pair (j,i) contributes to c_{i-j}, term h_j(1-h_i). Changing h_i second index: old 1, new 0 => delta -h_j = -1. That's one effect. Changing h_j first index? But h_j is first index in same pair; changing from1 to0: old h_j(1-h_i)=1, new 0 also delta -1. But these are not separate; the pair term changes once, not twice. When both endpoints change, we cannot sum first and second endpoint changes separately for the same pair because the term has product of both; there are two contributions in derivative but finite change is not linear sum. Need exact for pair (i,j): old term lag j-i: h_i(1-h_j)=0, new=1 => +1. old lag i-j: h_j(1-h_i)=1, new=0 => -1. For q not i,j, pairs involving i or j only one endpoint changes, finite delta linear formulas as above. So swap delta:

- lag q-i: +(1 - h_q)
- lag i-q: -h_q
- lag q-j: -(1 - h_q)
- lag j-q: +h_q
For pair i-j:
- lag j-i: +1
- lag i-j: -1
```

```
- second effect: delta[n-1-d] -= h[i-d] for left q; delta[n-1+d] -= h[i+d] for right q.
So add pattern vector can be built with slices O(1) Python per piece, not per q! Great.

Let's write functions:
```
def swap_delta(h, i, j, n):
    M = 2*n - 1
    d = M
    base = n-1
    delta = np.zeros(M)
    # add i (0->1)
    # first effect: lag q-i, for q!=i:
    # right q=i+1..n-1 offsets 1..n-1-i
    if i+1 < n:
        delta[base+1:base+n-i] += 1.0 - h[i+1:n]
    if i > 0:
        offs = 1..i; lag i-q = -d? For q=i-d, lag q-i = -d -> index base-d.
        delta[base-i:base][::-1] += 1.0 - h[i-1::-1]
    # second effect: lag i-q = d for right q; lag i-q = -? For q right (q=i+d): lag i-q = -d -> index base-d, value -h_q
    if i+1 < n:
        delta[base+1:base+n-i] -= h[i+1:n]
    if i > 0:
        delta[base-i:base][::-1] -= h[i-1::-1]
```
Check left first effect: q=i-d, lag q-i=-d index base-d; need assign for d=1..i, array h[i-d] = h[i-1::-1]; indices base-1,...,base-i should correspond d=1..i. `delta[base-i:base][::-1]` has elements indices base-1,base-2,...,base-i, matching h[i-1],h[i-2],... Good. Right first: d=1..n-1-i, indices base+1.. base+n-1-i, add h[i+1...]. Good.

```

  target=n/2

```
**Projected gradient polish**
Maybe after SLSQP, custom `gradient_polish` with high beta can squeeze. It can run many small steps until no improvement. Because F soft, gradient zero at optimum? We can implement but maybe not necessary. Could use to improve tiny. Let's design robust simple:
```
def steepest_descent(h,beta,deadline, max_iter=5000):
  n=...
  h=project
  F=soft max F
  alpha=0.1
  for it in range(max_iter):
    if time>deadline break
    F,qp,qn=calc
    g=grad
    # center gradient (? subtract mean to align sum)
    g = g - g.mean() # to help sum, though projection handles
    # generate y candidates with alpha and 0.5 alpha maybe
    improved=False
    for trial in range(5):
       y=project(h - alpha*g,n)
       Fy=calc(y)[0]
       if Fy < F - 1e-14:
          h=y; F=Fy; alpha=min(2*alpha,0.5); improved=True; break
       alpha*=0.5
    if not improved:
       # try very small step or break
       if alpha < 1e-5: break
       alpha=1e-4
  return h, hard_c
```
But line search computing calc multiple times expensive. Use hard C? F soft. Since beta high, F close. Need maybe not.

**Binary local search implementation**
Let's define `corr_signed(h,n)`:
```
M=2n-1; c=np.zeros(M); base=n-1
# compute c_s for s=0..n-1 pos, and for negative using neg
for l...
 c[base]=pos0
 for l in 1..n-1: c[base+l]=pos[l]; c[base-l]=neg[l]
return c
```
We can use compute_pos_neg already. For binary, values integer.
`binary_best_improve(h,n,deadline,max_passes=200)`:
```
h = to_binary(h)
corr=corr_signed(h,n)
cur=corr.max()*dx; best=cur; best_h=h.copy()
zeros = list(np.where(h==0)[0]); ones=list(np.where(h==1)[0])
for pass in range(max_passes):
  if time>deadline break
  improved=False
  # To avoid O(n^2) every pass too long, maybe use all pairs best.
  best_c=cur+1e-9; bi=bj=-1
  # Could subsample if many: random 5000 pairs? But best all okay.
  for i in zeros:
    # optional break if time
    for j in ones:
      if time: break loops maybe
      delta=swap_delta(h,i,j,n)
      nc = (corr + delta).max() * dx
      if nc < best_c:
         best_c=nc; bi=i; bj=j
  if bi!=-1:
     delta=swap_delta(h,bi,bj,n)
     corr=corr+delta
     h[bi]=1; h[bj]=0
     cur=best_c
     # update lists remove/append
     ones.remove(bj); zeros.remove(bi); ones.append(bi); zeros.append(bj)
     improved=True
     if cur<best: best=cur; best_h=h.copy()
     # integerize corr maybe
     if pass%10==0: corr=np.rint(corr)
  else:
     break
return best_h,best
```
This accepts only improvements (greedy). With all pairs, first improvement each pass (best among pairs). Could get stuck but okay.
```

```
**Better gradient and step**

Let's consider using **Newton/semidefinite**? no.

We can implement **projected BFGS/L-BFGS** using `scipy.optimize.minimize(method='L-BFGS-B')` but with a penalty for sum and rely on large penalty, because L-BFGS-B fast and robust with gradient. Parameterize h directly, objective = softF + λ (sum-target)^2, bounds (0,1). Start feasible. L-BFGS-B will try maintain penalty. Since equality hard, use large λ (e.g. 1000). But objective nonconvex; should work for local. Then after each run project. This may be faster than SLSQP and handle many iterations. However penalty may allow sum drift; but final projection may change C slightly. We can do adaptive λ and small penalty active.

L-BFGS-B with gradient:
```
def penalty_objective(x):
  h=np.clip(x,0,1)
  F=q_soft(h)
  s=h.sum()-target
  return F + lambda*s*s
def penalty_grad(x):
  g=softgrad
  g += 2 lambda * s * np.ones(n)
```
This doesn't enforce exactly but with high λ and small steps. Since gradient includes constant, okay. L-BFGS-B many iterations maybe thousands; can be faster than SLSQP. But equality constraint violation could limit. We can after run project and evaluate. We can use several λ increasing. This is a good alternative/complement.

Could also use `minimize` with method='L-BFGS-B' and `maxiter=1000`, `maxfun=5000`. n=400, gradient O(n^2) loops. Maybe 1000 eval per lambda. Good.

Potential issue: L-BFGS-B with bounds and nonconvex may converge to same. But we can combine: run L-BFGS-B for lower beta to explore, then SLSQP high beta for exact equality.

Alternatively use **FminL-BFGS-B** from scipy.

Let's design top-level `lbfgsb_refine(h,beta,deadline,maxiter,penalty)`. It uses global beta,penalty. Objective/grad with projection? We can evaluate on h as is (bounds handled by L-BFGS), no clipping? It respects bounds but can land exactly bounds. Sum can drift. Use penalty. It doesn't project; after call project. Could during objective if h slightly out of bounds due internal? L-BFGS keeps within bounds. Use `np.clip` just in case but then gradient mismatch if out; bounds should keep. Let's implement `signed_corr(h,n)`:
```
def signed_corr(h,n):
    M=2*n-1
    N=1<<(M-1).bit_length()
    H=np.fft.rfft(h,N)
    B=np.fft.rfft(1.0-h,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(H*np.conj(B),N)
    pos=c[:n]
    neg=np.empty(n)
    neg[0]=pos[0]
    if n>1: neg[1:]=c[n-1:0:-1]  # c[n-1],...,c[1]
    return pos,neg
```

**Projection**
Implement efficient `project_feasible(x,n)`. Need target = n/2.

```
def project_feasible(x,n):
    target=n*0.5
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    # if already close, fine
    lo = x.min() - 1.0
    hi = x.max()
    # actually tau range [x.min()-1, x.max()] works: at tau=lo, clip >=1? If x_i - lo >=1? lo=x.min()-1 => x_i-lo≥x.min()-x.min()+1=1 for min, >1 others -> all 1 sum n. At hi=x.max(), x_i-hi≤0 for max, others <0 -> all 0 sum 0.
    for _ in range(60):
       mid=(lo+hi)*0.5
       s=np.clip(x-mid,0,1).sum()
       if s > target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    y=np.clip(x-(lo+hi)*0.5,0,1)
    # adjust tiny sum
    diff=target-y.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-14:
       # distribute to non-bound?
       if diff>0:
          idx=np.argmin(y)?? If all 1 no room, but target<=n.
       ...
    return y
```
Alternatively solve via sorting O(n log n) but bisection fine. 60*400=24k ops.
```

```
**Optimize_resolution with L-BFGS-B + SLSQP?**

Given fast FFT, we can run custom **L-BFGS-B** with equality penalty and maybe it converges well. Let's design main `continuous_opt(h,beta,n,deadline, maxiter, penalty)` using `minimize(method='L-BFGS-B')`.

Objective/gradient with globals beta, n, N, penalty, mode? We can use top-level `_G` globals. But avoid lambdas. For L-BFGS-B, objective function signature `fun(x)`, jac `jac(x)`. We can set globals and define:
```
def lbfgs_fun(x):
   F,_,_ = calc_soft(x, _G['beta'], _G['n'], _G['N'])
   s = x.sum() - _G['target']
   return F + _G['pen']*s*s
def lbfgs_jac(x):
   F, qp,qn = calc_soft(...)
   g = grad_from_weights(x, qp,qn, n,N)
   g += 2*_G['pen']*(x.sum()-target)*np.ones(n)
   return g
```
Bounds [(0,1)]*n. `minimize(lbfgs_fun, x, jac=lbfgs_jac, bounds=bounds, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'ftol':1e-12, 'maxls':40})`.
L-BFGS-B doesn't support maxfun? options include maxfun. Use `maxfun=10000`.
After call, project.
Penalty: If pen too high, gradient constant large dominates; step small. Start pen=0 or small, increase. Since L-BFGS-B can handle drift, but equality exactly not. We can instead at each objective project h and ignore x? Not. Maybe we can use `SLSQP` for equality; L-BFGS-B penalty for exploration.
```

```
**Testing formulas mentally**
Let's verify `calc_soft`: For n=400, pos/neg arrays computed via FFT with h maybe not exactly sum n/2. Soft F independent. The gradient formula derived for c_l exact with constraints? It assumes c_l = ∑ over valid h_i(1-h_{i+l}). For pos l, derivative linear part: 1 for j ≤ n-1-l. For neg l: 1 for j ≥ l. Our L via cumulative uses qpos and qneg weights. L_j = ∑_{l=0}^{n-1-j} qpos[l] + ∑_{l=1}^{j} qneg[l]. Let's code:
```
cp = np.cumsum(qpos)  # cp[i]=sum qpos[0..i]
L = cp[::-1].copy()  # length n: [sum all, sum qpos[0..n-2], ..., qpos[0]] for j=0? Wait cp[::-1][0]=cp[n-1]=sum all; [1]=cp[n-2]=sum qpos[0..n-2]. Desired j=1 sum l=0..n-2 yes. Good. j=n-1 [n-1]=cp[0]=qpos0. Good.
cn = np.cumsum(qneg)  # [0, qn1, qn1+qn2,...]
L += cn  # j=0 qneg0=0; j=1 qn1; j=n-1 sum qn1..qnn-1. Good.
```
Thus L correct.

Kernel K: K0 = 2 qpos0. coeff d = qpos[d]+qneg[d]. For d=1..n-1. Build Kc as above. `T` via cross-correlation. Check for qpos+qneg normalized? Total qpos + qneg sum =1 (excluding neg0). K sum? Fine.
```

```
**SLSQP with gradient implementation details**
```
def slsqp_refine(h0, beta, n, N, maxiter, deadline=None, ftol=1e-12):
    _G['beta']=beta; _G['n']=n; _G['N']=N; _G['target']=n*0.5; _G['pen']=0.0
    x0=project_feasible(h0,n)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)] * n  # generated list, not literal; okay (list length not numeric literal)
    try:
       res = minimize(opt_fun, x0, method='SLSQP', jac=opt_jac, bounds=bounds,
                      constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':slsqp_cons,'jac':slsqp_cons_jac}],
                      options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':ftol})
       return res.x
    except Exception:
       return x0
```
Can't pass lambda, top-level functions. Good.
Check if `minimize` imports: `from scipy.optimize import minimize`.

**L-BFGS-B refine**
```
def lbfgsb_refine(h0,beta,pen,n,N,maxiter,deadline):
   _G betas pen
   x0=project_feasible(h0,n)
   bounds=[(0,1)]*n
   try:
      res=minimize(opt_fun,x0,method='L-BFGS-B',jac=opt_jac,bounds=bounds,
                   options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12,'maxfun':maxiter*10,'maxls':30})
      return res.x
   except: return x0
```
No constraint; project after.
```

```
**Implementation details for project_feasible**
```
def project_feasible(x,n):
    target=float(n)*0.5
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
    if x.shape[0]!=n: x=x[:n]
    lo=x.min()-1.0
    hi=x.max()
    for _ in range(60):
        mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
        s=np.clip(x-mid,0.0,1.0).sum()
        if s>target: lo=mid
        else: hi=mid
    y=np.clip(x-0.5*(lo+hi),0.0,1.0)
    diff=target-y.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-13:
        # adjust using small uniform over all? If within [0,1].
        # choose indices not saturated: if diff>0 add to where y<1 with smallest y; else subtract from y>0 with largest.
        if diff>0:
            idx=int(np.argmin(y))
        else:
            idx=int(np.argmax(y))
        if 0<=y[idx]-diff<=1:
            y[idx]+=diff
        else:
            # fallback uniform tiny
            y += diff/float(n)
            y=np.clip(y,0,1)
    return y
```
If target exact n/2 but due float diff maybe 1e-14; adjust. Good.

**`interp_parent`**
```
def interp_parent(parent,n,n0):
   if n==n0: return parent.copy()
   x0=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n0)
   x1=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n)
   return np.interp(x1,x0,parent)
```
If parent length different? pass n0.

**`make_resolutions`**
```
def make_resolutions(n0):
  s=set()
  fracs=[0.15,0.2,0.25,0.3,0.35,0.45,0.55,0.65,0.8,0.9,1.0,1.1,1.25,1.5,2.0]
  for f in fracs:
    m=int(round(n0*f/2.0)*2)
    if 48<=m<=900: s.add(m)
  for m in [64,80,96,128,160,192,224,256,320,384,448,512]:
    if 48<=m<=900: s.add(m)
  s.add(int(round(n0/2.0)*2))
  if len(s)%2: s.remove(min(s)) # not needed
  return sorted(s)
```
Too many? Up to maybe 20. Quick eval cheap. We will only optimize top few by dynamic weights.

**`optimize_resolution`**
Need use `time` globally? import time.
```
def optimize_resolution(h0,n,deadline,seed=42):
    n=int(n)
    h=project_feasible(h0,n)
    N=fft_len(n)
    c=hard_c5_np(h,n)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c
    now=time.time()
    rem=deadline-now
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed+n)
    # Phase 1 L-BFGS broad
    # total time budget for phase: use fractions but leave 20 for final SLSQP
    t1=now+0.35*rem
    lbf_tasks=[(20.0,0.0,300),(50.0,1.0,300),(100.0,10.0,300),(200.0,50.0,300),(500.0,200.0,300)]
    for beta,pen,mi in lbf_tasks:
       if time.time()>t1-1: break
       # if n large reduce mi
       if n>500: mi=min(mi,150)
       h2=lbfgsb_refine(h,beta,pen,n,N,mi,t1-1)
       h2=project_feasible(h2,n)
       c2=hard_c5_np(h2,n)
       if c2 < best_c + 1e-12:
           if c2 < best_c:
              best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy()
           h=h2
       # else keep h? Maybe keep h2 if not much worse? Use if c2 < best_c + 0.002
       elif c2 < best_c + 0.002:
           h=h2
    # Phase 2 SLSQP exact
    n_betas=8
    t2=now+0.85*rem
    betas=[100,200,500,1000,2000,4000,8000,15000]
    base_iter=300 if n<=128 else 200 if n<=320 else 100
    for i,beta in enumerate(betas):
       if time.time()>t2-2: break
       # scale iter by time left and beta? 
       mi=base_iter
       if beta>5000: mi=max(50, base_iter//2)
       h2=slsqp_refine(h,beta,n,N,mi,t2-2)
       h2=project_feasible(h2,n)
       c2=hard_c5_np(h2,n)
       if c2 < best_c:
          best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy(); h=h2
       elif c2 < best_c + 0.001:
          h=h2
       # optional if no improvement and h2 worse, reset h=best_h? 
    # Phase3 final high beta polish from best_h
    t3=deadline-2
    if time.time()<t3-5:
       h=best_h.copy()
       for beta in [8000,15000,30000]:
          if time.time()>t3-2: break
          h2=slsqp_refine(h,beta,n,N,80 if n>300 else 150,t3-2)
          h2=project_feasible(h2,n); c2=hard_c5_np(h2,n)
          if c2<best_c: best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy(); h=h2
          else: h=best_h.copy()
    # Phase4 noise restarts if time
    if time.time()<deadline-8:
      noises=[0.002,0.005,0.01,0.02]
      for idx,ns in enumerate(noises):
         if time.time()>deadline-4: break
         h2=best_h + ns*rng.randn(n)
         h2=project_feasible(h2,n)
         h2=slsqp_refine(h2,5000,n,N,60 if n>300 else 120, deadline-0.5)
         h2=project_feasible(h2,n); c2=hard_c5_np(h2,n)
         if c2<best_c: best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy()
    return best_h,best_c,n
```

Detailed code:

```
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

_G = {}
_OPT_CACHE = None? No.

def _fft_len(n):
    m = 2*n - 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()

def project_feasible(x, n):
    target = float(n) * 0.5
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float).reshape(-1)
    if x.size != n:
        x = np.resize(x, n)
    lo = float(x.min()) - 1.0
    hi = float(x.max())
    for _ in range(50):
        mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
        s = np.clip(x-mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum()
        if s > target: lo = mid
        else: hi = mid
    y = np.clip(x - 0.5*(lo+hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = target - float(y.sum())
    if abs(diff) > 1e-14:
        if diff > 0:
            idx = int(np.argmin(y))
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(y))
        val = y[idx] + diff
        if 0.0 <= val <= 1.0:
            y[idx] = val
        else:
            y += diff / float(n)
            y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)
    return y
```
Projection bisection 50 enough. If x.size mismatch after resize, sum maybe? okay.

`to_binary` maybe not used; skip.

`interp_parent`:
```
def interp_parent(parent, n, n0):
    if n == n0:
        return parent.copy()
    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n0)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n)
    return np.interp(x1, x0, parent)
```

`signed_corr_fft`:
```
def signed_corr_fft(h, n, N=None):
    if N is None: N=_fft_len(n)
    H = np.fft.rfft(h, N)
    B = np.fft.rfft(1.0 - h, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(H * np.conj(B), N)
    pos = c[:n].copy()
    neg = np.empty(n)
    neg[0] = pos[0]
    if n > 1:
        neg[1:] = c[N-1:N-n:-1]
    return pos, neg
```
Check slice `c[N-1:N-n:-1]` length n-1. For n=2,N=4, c[3:2:-1] length1. Good.

`calc_soft`:
```
def calc_soft(x, beta, n, N):
    h = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    pos, neg = signed_corr_fft(h,n,N)
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    vp = pos * dx
    vpmax = float(vp.max())
    if n > 1:
        vnmax = float(neg[1:].max())
        if vnmax > vpmax: vpmax = vnmax
    epos = beta * (vp - vpmax)
    epos = np.clip(epos, -745.0, 0.0)
    ep = np.exp(epos)
    en = np.zeros(n)
    if n > 1:
        eneg = beta * (neg[1:] * dx - vpmax)
        eneg = np.clip(eneg, -745.0, 0.0)
        en[1:] = np.exp(eneg)
    tot = float(ep.sum() + en.sum())
    if tot <= 0: tot=1.0
    qpos = ep / tot
    qneg = en / tot
    F = vpmax + float(np.log(tot)) / beta
    return F, qpos, qneg
```
`vpmax` should consider `neg[0]` same as vp[0]; included. Good.

`grad_from_weights`:
```
def grad_from_weights(x, qpos, qneg, n, N):
    h = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    dx = 2.0/float(n)
    L = np.cumsum(qpos)[::-1].copy()
    L += np.cumsum(qneg)
    Kc = np.zeros(N)
    Kc[0] = 2.0 * qpos[0]
    if n > 1:
        coeff = qpos[1:] + qneg[1:]
        Kc[1:n] = coeff
        Kc[N-1:N-n:-1] = coeff
    H = np.fft.rfft(h, N)
    Kf = np.fft.rfft(Kc, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(H * np.conj(Kf), N)
    T = np.empty(n)
    T[0] = c[0]
    if n > 1:
        T[1:] = c[N-1:N-n:-1]
    return dx * (L - T)
```
Check if L length n: np.cumsum(qneg) length n, qneg[0]=0. Good. `Kc[N-1:N-n:-1]` if N-n maybe 0? For n=1 no. For n=64,N=256? N-n=192 positive. Good. If N == n? Not; N≥2n-1 > n for n>1.

`opt_fun`:
```
def opt_fun(x):
    h = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    beta = _G['beta']; n=_G['n']; N=_G['N']; pen=_G['pen']
    F,_,_ = calc_soft(h,beta,n,N)
    s = float(h.sum() - _G['target'])
    return F + pen*s*s
```
`opt_jac`:
```
def opt_jac(x):
    h = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    beta = _G['beta']; n=_G['n']; N=_G['N']; pen=_G['pen']
    F, qp, qn = calc_soft(h,beta,n,N)
    g = grad_from_weights(h, qp, qn, n, N)
    if pen != 0.0:
        s = float(h.sum() - _G['target'])
        g = g + (2.0*pen*s)*np.ones(n)
    return g
```
No cache.

`slsqp_cons`, `slsqp_cons_jac`:
```
def slsqp_cons(x): return float(np.asarray(x).sum() - _G['target'])
def slsqp_cons_jac(x): return np.ones(np.asarray(x).shape, dtype=float)
```

`slsqp_refine`:
```
def slsqp_refine(h0, beta, n, N, maxiter, deadline):
    _G['beta']=float(beta); _G['n']=int(n); _G['N']=int(N); _G['target']=float(n)*0.5; _G['pen']=0.0
    x0 = project_feasible(h0,n)
    bounds = [(0.0,1.0)] * n
    cons = [{'type':'eq','fun':slsqp_cons,'jac':slsqp_cons_jac}]
    try:
        res = minimize(opt_fun, x0, method='SLSQP', jac=opt_jac, bounds=bounds, constraints=cons,
                       options={'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'ftol': 1e-12})
        x = res.x
    except Exception:
        x = x0
    return project_feasible(x,n)
```
`deadline` not used; could if time before? We can check before call. SLSQP no interrupt. `maxiter` small.

`lbfgsb_refine`:
```
def lbfgsb_refine(h0,beta,pen,n,N,maxiter,deadline):
    _G... pen
    x0=project_feasible(h0,n)
    bounds=[(0,1)]*n
    try:
       res=minimize(opt_fun,x0,method='L-BFGS-B',jac=opt_jac,bounds=bounds,
                    options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-12,'maxfun':int(maxiter*12),'maxls':30})
       x=res.x
    except: x=x0
    return project_feasible(x,n)
```

`hard_c5_fft`:
```
def hard_c5_fft(h,n):
    N=_fft_len(n)
    pos,neg=signed_corr_fft(h,n,N)
    return float(max(pos.max(), neg.max())*2.0/float(n))
```
Could precompute N in optimize; pass.
`hard_c5_np`:
```
def hard_c5_np(h,n):
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')) * 2.0/float(n))
```

`make_resolutions` as above.

`optimize_resolution` refined.
Let's write more precise tasks and h_work selection.

```
def optimize_resolution(h0,n,deadline,seed=42):
    n=int(n); h0=np.asarray(h0,float)
    h=project_feasible(h0,n)
    N=_fft_len(n)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=hard_c5_np(h,n)
    now=time.time(); rem=max(0.0, deadline-now)
    if rem <= 2: return best_h,best_c,n
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed+n*10007)
    # choose h_work
    h_work=best_h.copy()
    # L-BFGS tasks
    if n <= 160: lbf=[(20,0,600),(50,0,600),(100,10,500),(300,100,400),(1000,1000,300)]
    else: lbf=[(20,0,400),(50,1,400),(100,10,300),(300,100,300),(1000,1000,200)]
    t_lbf = now + 0.35*rem
    for beta,pen,mi in lbf:
       if time.time() > t_lbf - 1: break
       if n > 500: mi = max(50, min(mi, 150))
       h2 = lbfgsb_refine(h_work, beta, pen, n, N, mi, t_lbf-1)
       c2 = hard_c5_np(h2,n)
       if c2 < best_c:
           best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy(); h_work=h
           h_work=h2.copy()
       elif c2 < best_c + 0.0015:
           h_work=h2.copy()
       # else keep previous h_work (or best?)
       if time.time() > t_lbf - 1: break
    # SLSQP tasks from best_h (not h_work if much worse)
    start = best_h.copy()
    if h_work is not None and hard_c5_np(h_work,n) < best_c + 0.0005:
         start = h_work.copy()
    t_sls = now + 0.85*rem
    if n <= 160: betas=[100,300,1000,3000,10000,30000]; base=200
    elif n <= 320: betas=[100,300,1000,3000,10000,30000]; base=120
    else: betas=[200,500,1500,5000,15000]; base=80
    for beta in betas:
       if time.time() > t_sls - 2: break
       mi = base
       if beta >= 10000: mi = max(40, base//2)
       h2 = slsqp_refine(start, beta, n, N, mi, t_sls-2)
       c2 = hard_c5_np(h2,n)
       if c2 < best_c:
           best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy(); start=h2.copy()
       elif c2 < best_c + 0.0005:
           start=h2.copy()
       else:
           start=best_h.copy()
    # final polish with L-BFGS high penalty from best
    if time.time() < deadline - 10:
       t_final = deadline - 2
       final_beta = 20000 if n>300 else 50000
       h2 = lbfgsb_refine(best_h, final_beta, 1000.0, n, N, 300, t_final)
       c2=hard_c5_np(h2,n)
       if c2<best_c: best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy()
       # SLSQP high
       h2 = slsqp_refine(best_h, final_beta, n, N, 80 if n>300 else 160, t_final)
       c2=hard_c5_np(h2,n)
       if c2<best_c: best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy()
    # noise restarts
    if time.time() < deadline - 6:
       noises=[0.001,0.003,0.008,0.02]
       for idx,ns in enumerate(noises):
          if time.time() > deadline - 3: break
          h2 = best_h + ns * rng.randn(n)
          h2 = project_feasible(h2,n)
          beta = 5000 if n>300 else 20000
          h2 = slsqp_refine(h2, beta, n, N, 60 if n>300 else 120, deadline-0.5)
          h2 = project_feasible(h2,n)
          c2=hard_c5_np(h2,n)
          if c2<best_c:
             best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy()
    return best_h,best_c,n

def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100,**kwargs):
  t0=time.time(); total=budget_s-12; deadline=t0+total

  best_h=h_parent; best_c=hard_c5_np(h_parent,n_parent); best_n=n_parent
  resols=make_resolutions(n_parent)
  # quick eval list (h, c, n)
  cand=[]
  for n in resols:
    if n<=0 or n%2: continue
    h=interp_parent(h_parent,n,n_parent) if n!=n_parent else h_parent
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    c=hard_c5_fft(h,n)  # quick
    cand.append((c,n,h))
  cand.sort(key=lambda t: t[0]) # lambda? No lambdas allowed! Need avoid. Use `cand.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))`? Need import operator? Or implement custom sort by tuple: cand.sort() since first element c. Use `cand.sort()`.
  weights=[30,12,6,3,2,1,1,1,1,1]
  remaining=deadline-time.time()
  idx=0
  for c0,n,h in cand:
     if idx>=len(weights): slice_w=0? break if time <20
     if time.time() > deadline - 30: break
     wsum=sum(weights[idx:]) if idx < len(weights) else 1
     if wsum <=0: break
     frac=weights[idx]/wsum
     slice=remaining*frac
     if slice < 12: slice=remaining*0.15 if remaining>20 else 0
     if slice <= 5: break
     t_end=time.time()+slice
     h2,c2,n2=optimize_resolution(h,n,t_end,seed+idx*101)
     if c2 < best_c:
        best_c=c2; best_h=h2; best_n=n2
     remaining=deadline-time.time()
     idx+=1
  # final verify and exact sum
  best_h=project_feasible(best_h,best_n)
  final_c=hard_c5_np(best_h,best_n)
  if final_c < best_c: best_c=final_c else best_c=final_c
  # if possible use evaluate? no
  return best_h,best_c,best_n

Implement `to_binary`:
```
def to_binary(h,n):
   k=n//2
   idx=np.argsort(h)[::-1]
   out=np.zeros(n); out[idx[:k]]=1.0; return out
```
For ties, arbitrary.

`swap_delta` function with h, i, j, n.
Let's write carefully:
```
def swap_delta(h, i, j, n):
    M=2*n-1; base=n-1
    d=np.zeros(M)
    R=n-1-i; L=i
    if R>0:
        seg=1.0 - h[i+1:]
        d[base+1:base+R+1] += seg
        d[base-R:base][::-1] -= h[i+1:]
    if L>0:
        seg=1.0 - h[i-1::-1]
        d[base-L:base][::-1] += seg
        d[base+1:base+L+1] -= h[i-1::-1]
    # remove j
    Rj=n-1-j; Lj=j
    if Rj>0:
        seg=1.0 - h[j+1:]
        d[base+1:base+Rj+1] -= seg
        d[base-Rj:base][::-1] += h[j+1:]
    if Lj>0:
        seg=1.0 - h[j-1::-1]
        d[base-Lj:base][::-1] -= seg
        d[base+1:base+Lj+1] += h[j-1::-1]
    d[base + j - i] += 1.0
    d[base + i - j] += 1.0
    return d
```
Check zero/one assumptions: i zero, j one. The add/remove formulas assume old h_i=0,h_j=1. If h is binary yes. If due float 0/1. Good.
But we used add pattern includes q=j and remove includes q=i, and correction +1 both. Good.
If i and j order, base± shifts within 0..M-1? |j-i|≤n-1, yes. If i==j never.

Test with n=3 earlier, should work.

`binary_c5_from_corr(corr,n)=corr.max()*2/n`.

`binary_local_search(h,n,deadline,seed, max_passes=1000)`:
```
def binary_local_search(h,n,deadline,seed):
   hb=to_binary(h,n)
   N=_fft_len(n); pos,neg=signed_corr_fft(hb,n,N); corr=...
   dx=2/n; cur=corr.max()*dx; best_h=hb.copy(); best_c=cur
   zeros=list(np.where(hb==0)[0]); ones=list(np.where(hb==1)[0])
   pass_count=0
   while time.time()<deadline and pass_count<1000:
       pass_count+=1
       best_sweep=cur + 1e-14
       bi=bj=-1
       ndelta=None
       # For n small, evaluate all pairs. For n larger, maybe sample if time.
       for ii,i in enumerate(zeros):
           if time.time()>deadline: break
           for jj,j in enumerate(ones):
               delta=swap_delta(hb,i,j,n)
               nc=(corr+delta).max()*dx
               if nc < best_sweep:
                   best_sweep=nc; bi=i; bj=j; ndelta=delta
       if bi==-1: break
       if ndelta is None:
           ndelta=swap_delta(hb,bi,bj,n)
       corr=corr+ndelta
       # Optional renormalize to binary integer? corr values floats; maybe round if n<=256 every 10 passes
       # update lists efficiently
       ones.remove(bj); zeros.remove(bi); ones.append(bi); zeros.append(bj)
       hb[bi]=1.0; hb[bj]=0.0
       cur=best_sweep
       if cur < best_c:
           best_c=cur; best_h=hb.copy()
   return best_h,best_c
```
Potential performance: For n=64, zeros/ones length32, pairs 1024. Delta allocation length127; fine. `ones.remove(bj)` O(n) fine. For n=200 pairs 10000, delta400; maybe ~? Each pass maybe 0.05s. Explore few passes.
We can stop binary search after no improve (local). For random basin, local may be high. Add **anneal** for low n to escape? Could implement random sweep accept if improves or with temperature.        accept...


**Compress project**:
```
def project_feasible(x,n):
 t=n/2; x=np.asarray(x,float).ravel()
 lo=x.min()-1; hi=x.max()
 for _ in range(45):
  m=(lo+hi)/2; s=np.clip(x-m,0,1).sum()
  if s>t: lo=m
  else: hi=m
 y=np.clip(x-(lo+hi)/2,0,1)
 d=t-y.sum()
 if d:
  i=np.argmin(y)+d if d>0 else ...` no, careful.
 return y
```

**Compress binary_search**:
```
def binary_local_search(h,n,deadline,seed):
 h=to_binary(h,n); N=_fft_len(n); pos,neg=signed_corr_fft(h,n,N); c=corr_from_pos_neg(pos,neg,n); dx=2/n; cur=c.max()*dx; best_c=cur; best=h.copy()
 z=list(np.where(h==0)[0]); o=list(np.where(h==1)[0])
 p=0
 while time.time()<deadline and p<500:
  p+=1; bc=cur+1e-12; bi=bj=-1; bd=None
  for i in z:
   for j in o:
    d=swap_delta(h,i,j,n); v=(c+d).max()*dx
    if v<bc: bc=v; bi=i; bj=j; bd=d
  if bi<0: break
  c=c+bd; h[bi]=1; h[bj]=0; o.remove(bj); z.remove(bi); o.append(bi); z.append(bj); cur=bc
  if cur<best_c: best_c=cur; best=h.copy()
  if p%20==0:
   pos,neg=signed_corr_fft(h,n,N); c=corr_from_pos_neg(pos,neg,n); cur=c.max()*dx
 return best,best_c
```
No anneal. Good.

Cross-correlation c[k]=∑ h_i Kc[(i+k) mod N]. Kc[0]=2, so c[0]=2∑h, c[k≠0]=0? Wait T_j should 2h_j (not 2∑h). Our formula T_j = c[N-j]. For Kc delta at 0, c[k]=∑ h_i 1_{(i+k) mod N =0} = h_i where i = -k mod N. For k=N-j, T_j = c[N-j]=h_j. Times 2 -> 2h_j. Good. I mistakenly thought c[0] only; c[k] nonzero for k corresponding. So correct.

**Potential issue: Kc for distances: Kc[1]=K1, Kc[N-1]=K1. For T_1: c[N-1]=∑ h_i Kc[(i-1) mod N]. i=0: Kc[N-1]=K1 for r? T_1 = h0 K1 + h1 K0 + h2 K1? Let's desired T_1 for j=1: sum_r h_r K[|r-1|]: r0 K1, r1 K0, r2 K1. Formula c[N-1] = ∑ h_i Kc[(i-1) mod N]: i=0 -> Kc[N-1]=K1 (r0); i=1 -> Kc[0]=K0; i=2 -> Kc[1]=K1. Good.

**Now, can we use `np.fft.rfft` on Kc each gradient with many zeros; okay.

**Potential issue: SLSQP with analytic gradient may ask for Hessian finite diff? It uses gradient; okay.

**Let's consider a simpler objective: Instead of soft logsum, use **hard max plus tiny regularization** and use SLSQP? SLSQP can handle nonconvex nonsmooth? No. Soft high beta okay.

**Add `np.errstate`? no.

**Could `np.exp` on array with -745 underflow warning? Using clip avoids underflow. But exp(-745) ~5e-324 maybe subnormal; okay. Use -700 to be safe. `np.clip(epos, -700.0, 0.0)`.

**Let's draft final code carefully.**

Pseudo final:
```
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

_G = {}

def _fft_len(n):
    m=2*n-1
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()

def project_feasible(x,n):
    t=0.5*float(n)
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float).ravel()
    if x.size != n:
       x=np.resize(x,n)
    lo=float(x.min())-1.0; hi=float(x.max())
    for _ in range(50):
       mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
       if np.clip(x-mid,0.0,1.0).sum() > t: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    y=np.clip(x-0.5*(lo+hi),0.0,1.0)
    d=t-float(y.sum())
    if abs(d)>1e-14:
       idx=int(np.argmin(y)) if d>0 else int(np.argmax(y))
       if 0.0 <= y[idx]+d <= 1.0: y[idx]+=d
       else: y+=d/float(n); y=np.clip(y,0.0,1.0)
    return y

def interp_parent(par,n,n0):
    if n==n0: return par.copy()
    return np.interp(np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n), np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n0), par)

def to_binary(h,n):
    idx=np.argsort(h)[::-1]
    b=np.zeros(n); b[idx[:n//2]]=1.0; return b

def signed_corr_fft(h,n,N=None):
    N=_fft_len(n) if N is None else N
    H=np.fft.rfft(h,N); B=np.fft.rfft(1.0-h,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(H*np.conj(B),N)
    pos=c[:n].copy()
    neg=np.empty(n); neg[0]=pos[0]
    if n>1: neg[1:]=c[N-1:N-n:-1]
    return pos,neg

def corr_from_pos_neg(pos,neg,n):
    c=np.empty(2*n-1); base=n-1
    c[base:]=pos
    if n>1: c[:base]=neg[1:][::-1]
    return c

def calc_soft(x,beta,n,N):
    h=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
    pos,neg=signed_corr_fft(h,n,N)
    dx=2.0/float(n)
    vp=pos*dx
    vmax=float(vp.max())
    if n>1 and float(neg[1:].max())>vmax: vmax=float(neg[1:].max())
    ep=np.exp(np.clip(beta*(vp-vmax),-700.0,0.0))
    en=np.zeros(n)
    if n>1:
       en[1:]=np.exp(np.clip(beta*(neg[1:]*dx-vmax),-700.0,0.0))
    tot=float(ep.sum()+en.sum())
    if tot<=0: tot=1.0
    return vmax+float(np.log(tot))/beta, ep/tot, en/tot

def grad_from_weights(x,qpos,qneg,n,N):
    h=np.asarray(x,dtype=float); dx=2.0/float(n)
    L=np.cumsum(qpos)[::-1].copy()
    L+=np.cumsum(qneg)
    K=np.zeros(N); K[0]=2.0*qpos[0]
    if n>1:
       cf=qpos[1:]+qneg[1:]
       K[1:n]=cf
       K[N-1:N-n:-1]=cf
    C=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(h,N)*np.conj(np.fft.rfft(K,N)),N)
    T=np.empty(n); T[0]=C[0]
    if n>1: T[1:]=C[N-1:N-n:-1]
    return dx*(L-T)

def opt_fun(x):
    beta=_G['beta']; n=int(_G['n']); N=int(_G['N']); pen=float(_G['pen'])
    F,_,_=calc_soft(x,beta,n,N)
    s=float(np.asarray(x).sum()-_G['target'])
    return F+pen*s*s

def opt_jac(x):
    beta=_G['beta']; n=int(_G['n']); N=int(_G['N']); pen=float(_G['pen'])
    F,qp,qn=calc_soft(x,beta,n,N)
    g=grad_from_weights(x,qp,qn,n,N)
    if pen:
       g=(g+(2.0*pen*(float(np.asarray(x).sum()-_G['target']))*np.ones(n)))
    return g

def cons_fun(x): return float(np.asarray(x).sum()-_G['target'])
def cons_jac(x): return np.ones(np.asarray(x).shape,dtype=float)

def slsqp_refine(h0,beta,n,N,maxiter):
    _G['beta']=float(beta); _G['n']=n; _G['N']=N; _G['target']=0.5*float(n); _G['pen']=0.0
    x0=project_feasible(h0,n)
    bnd=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':cons_fun,'jac':cons_jac}]
    try:
       res=minimize(opt_fun,x0,method='SLSQP',jac=opt_jac,bounds=bnd,constraints=cons,options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-12})
       x=res.x
    except Exception:
       x=x0
    return project_feasible(x,n)

def lbfgsb_refine(h0,beta,pen,n,N,maxiter):
    _G['beta']=float(beta); _G['n']=n; _G['N']=N; _G['target']=0.5*float(n); _G['pen']=float(pen)
    x0=project_feasible(h0,n)
    bnd=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    try:
       res=minimize(opt_fun,x0,method='L-BFGS-B',jac=opt_jac,bounds=bnd,options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-12,'maxfun':int(max(100,maxiter*12)),'maxls':30})
       x=res.x
    except Exception:
       x=x0
    return project_feasible(x,n)

def hard_c5_fft(h,n):
    pos,neg=signed_corr_fft(h,n,_fft_len(n))
    return float(max(float(pos.max()),float(neg.max()))*2.0/float(n))

def hard_c5_np(h,n):
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full'))*2.0/float(n))

def swap_delta(h,i,j,n):
    base=n-1; d=np.zeros(2*n-1)
    R=n-1-i; L=i
    if R:
       d[base+1:base+R+1]+=1.0-h[i+1:]
       d[base-R:base][::-1]-=h[i+1:]
    if L:
       d[base-L:base][::-1]+=1.0-h[i-1::-1]
       d[base+1:base+L+1]-=h[i-1::-1]
    R=n-1-j; L=j
    if R:
       d[base+1:base+R+1]-=1.0-h[j+1:]
       d[base-R:base][::-1]+=h[j+1:]
    if L:
       d[base-L:base][::-1]-=1.0-h[j-1::-1]
       d[base+1:base+L+1]+=h[j-1::-1]
    d[base+j-i]+=1.0
    d[base+i-j]+=1.0
    return d

def binary_local_search(h,n,deadline,seed):
    h=to_binary(h,n)
    N=_fft_len(n)
    pos,neg=signed_corr_fft(h,n,N)
    c=corr_from_pos_neg(pos,neg,n)
    dx=2.0/float(n)
    cur=float(c.max())*dx
    best_c=cur; best=h.copy()
    z=list(np.where(h==0.0)[0]); o=list(np.where(h==1.0)[0])
    p=0
    while time.time()<deadline and p<500:
       p+=1; bc=cur+1e-12; bi=-1; bd=None
       for i in z:
          for j in o:
             d=swap_delta(h,i,j,n)
             v=float((c+d).max())*dx
             if v<bc:
                bc=v; bi=i; bd=d
       if bi<0: break
       c=c+bd
       h[bi]=1.0; h[o[0] if False else o.remove? ...]
```
Need update lists when finding both i and j. In code above, I lost bj. Store `bj=j`. Update:
```
 for i in z:
   for j in o:
      ...
      if v<bc: bc=v; bi=i; bj=j; bd=d
...
 c+=bd; h[bi]=1; h[bj]=0
 o.remove(bj); z.remove(bi); o.append(bi); z.append(bj)
 cur=bc
```
Good.
Performance: The nested loop uses Python for each pair; for n=200 10k pairs; okay. In `swap_delta`, `if R:` where R int. Good.

Potential note: In binary search, we assign `bd` but when best found, `bd` corresponds to h state at evaluation. Since h not changed during sweep, okay. After sweep, apply. If `bd` huge memory; okay.

`make_resolutions`:
```
def make_resolutions(n0):
 s=set()
 for f in (0.15,0.2,0.25,0.3,0.4,0.5,0.6,0.75,0.9,1.0,1.25,1.5):
   m=2*int(round(n0*f/2.0));
   if 64<=m<=800: s.add(m)
 for m in (64,80,96,128,160,224,256,320,384,448,512,640):
   s.add(m)
 s.add(2*int(round(max(64,n0)/2.0)))
 return sorted(list(s))
```
Ensure no m<64.

`optimize_resolution`:
Need compact and effective.
```
def optimize_resolution(h0,n,deadline,seed):
 n=int(n); h=project_feasible(h0,n); N=_fft_len(n)
 best_h=h.copy(); best_c=hard_c5_np(h,n)
 now=time.time(); rem=deadline-now
 if rem<10: return best_h,best_c,n
 rng=np.random.RandomState(seed*100003+n)
 # binary
 if n<=200:
    tb=now+min(0.25*rem, 150.0 if n<=128 else 70.0)
    hb,cb=binary_local_search(h,n,tb,seed)
    if cb<best_c: best_c=cb; best_h=hb.copy()
    if n<=128 and time.time()<tb-20:
       hr=rng.rand(n)
       hb,cb=binary_local_search(to_binary(hr,n),tb,seed+1)
       if cb<best_c: best_c=cb; best_h=hb.copy()
 # continuous
 rem=deadline-time.time()
 if rem>20:
    t_lb=time.time()+0.35*rem
    tasks=[]
    if n<=128: tasks=[(10,10,800),(50,50,600),(200,500,500)]
    elif n<=256: tasks=[(20,20,500),(100,100,400),(500,1000,300)]
    else: tasks=[(50,100,300),(200,1000,250)]
    for beta,pen,mi in tasks:
       if time.time()>t_lb-1: break
       h2=lbfgsb_refine(best_h if n>300 else best_h,beta,pen,n,N,mi)
       c2=hard_c5_np(h2,n)
       if c2<best_c: best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy()
       elif c2<best_c+0.001: best_h=h2.copy()
    start=best_h.copy()
    rem=deadline-time.time()
    if rem>15:
       t_sl=time.time()+0.80*rem
       if n<=96:
          betas=(100,300,1000,5000,20000); base=500
       elif n<=200:
          betas=(200,1000,5000,20000); base=250
       elif n<=400:
          betas=(500,2000,10000); base=150
       else:
          betas=(1000,5000); base=80
       for beta in betas:
          if time.time()>t_sl-2: break
          mi=base
          if beta>10000: mi=max(60,base//2)
          h2=slsqp_refine(start,beta,n,N,mi)
          c2=hard_c5_np(h2,n)
          if c2<best_c:
             best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy(); start=h2.copy()
          elif c2<best_c+0.0005:
             start=h2.copy()
          else:
             start=best_h.copy()
 # final polish
 if time.time()<deadline-8:
    bf=30000 if n<=200 else 10000
    h2=slsqp_refine(best_h,bf,n,N,200 if n<=128 else 80)
    c2=hard_c5_np(h2,n)
    if c2<best_c: best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy()
 # noise
 if time.time()<deadline-5:
    for ns in (0.002,0.006,0.02):
       if time.time()>deadline-3: break
       h2=project_feasible(best_h+ns*rng.randn(n),n)
       h2=slsqp_refine(h2,10000 if n>200 else 30000,n,N,80 if n>200 else 160)
       c2=hard_c5_np(h2,n)
       if c2<best_c: best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy()
 return best_h,best_c,n
```
This calls L-BFGS from best_h. For n low, continuous from binary best. But if best_h is binary and c2 from L-BFGS not improve, `best_h` remains binary. SLSQP then from binary. Good.

However, L-BFGS with beta=10 pen=10 may try reduce soft objective but equality; okay. But if start is binary and beta low, it may move to fractional.

If all y=1 and target <n impossible because target n/2 <n; if all y=1, sum n > target, bisection would not all 1? But due rounding maybe. Check `if 0<=y[idx]+d<=1` else uniform. If uniform makes out, clip; sum may not exact but tiny. okay.

Double-check `binary_local_search`: `bd` may be None if no improve; break. If best found, store `bj`. When updating lists, `o.remove(bj)` O(n) but list elements ints. `z.remove(bi)`. Good. If due floating h[bi] not exactly 0? binary yes. In random start to_binary exact.

Double-check `swap_delta` with i/j positions and correction: The correction adds +1 at both lags. What about if the pair (i,j) lags already got contributions from loops including q=i/j. We tested one case. Let's test opposite orientation i>j example to be sure.
n=3 h=[0,1,0], i=2 zero, j=1 one. Swap to [0,0,1]. Old: c_s? h0=0,h1=1,h2=0. c [s] signed:
s=-1: h1(1-h0)=1, h2(1-h1)=0 =>1
s=0:0, s=1: h0(1-h1)=0,h1(1-h2)=1 =>1, s=-2: h2(1-h0)=0, s=2: h0?0.
New h=[0,0,1]: s=1: h0=0,h1(1-h2)=0 =>0; s=-1: h1(1-h0)=0,h2(1-h1)=1 =>1; s=2: h0(1-h2)=0; s=-2: h2(1-h0)=1. So delta: s=1 -1, s=-1 0, s=2 0? old s2 0 new0; s=-2 +1.
Our code: i=2,j=1, base=2.
Add i=2: R=0,L=2. left q=1: `seg=1-h[1::-1]=1-h1=0`; d[base-2:base][::-1]=d[0:2][::-1] indices1,0 +=0; d[base+1:base+2+1]=d3:5? length2 indices3,4 -=h[1::-1]=[1,0] -> idx3 -1, idx4 0.
Remove j=1: R=1,L=1.
Rq=2: seg=1-h2=1; d[base+1:base+2]=idx3 -=1; d[base-1:base][::-1]=idx1 +=h2=0.
Lq=0: seg=1-h0=1; d[base-1:base][::-1]=idx1 -=1; d[base+1:base+2]=idx3 +=h0=0.
Corrections: d[base+j-i]=d[2-1]=idx1 +=1; d[base+i-j]=d[2+1]=idx3 +=1.
Totals: idx1: add0 + rem(-1) + corr1 =0 (s=-1 delta0). idx3: add -1 + rem(idx3 -1) + corr1 = -1 (s=1 delta -1). idx? s=-2 idx0 none? Wait exact expected s=-2 +1. Our code got none. Did we miss? Let's recalc exact new/old for s=-2 (lag -2): c_{-2}=∑_{i=2}^{2} h_i(1-h_{i-2})=h2(1-h0). Old h2=0,h0=0 =>0; new h2=1,h0=0 =>1. Delta +1 at lag -2 index base-2=0. Our code didn't produce idx0. Where should it come? Pair i=2 (zero→one), j=1 (one→zero). For q not i,j: q=0. Contributions for q=0:
- add i first effect lag q-i = -2: +(1-h0)=+1 -> idx0. In code add i left: q=0 is i-d with d=2. We did left first: `d[base-L:base][::-1] += seg` where seg=1-h[i-1::-1] = h[1],h[0]? Wait i=2, i-1::-1 yields indices 1,0 values [1,0]; 1- = [0,1]. Slice `d[base-L:base][::-1]` = d[0:2][::-1] indices 1,0. We assigned seg [0,1], so idx0 +=1! I earlier said +=0 erroneously because I thought seg=1-h1 only. It includes both q=1 and q=0. So idx0 +1. Then remove j left q=0: segment 1-h[0]=1, slice idx1 only? Lj=1, j=1, j-1::-1=[0], only q=0 for j left; no idx0. So idx0 +1. Good. idx1 recomputed: add left q=1 seg0 idx1; remove j right? q=2 idx? **Potential issue: In binary search, `bd` has float values maybe not integer; correction etc. fine.

```python
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

_G = {}

def _fft_len(n):
    m = 2 * int(n) - 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()

def project_feasible(x, n):
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float).ravel()
    if x.size != n:
        x = np.resize(x, n)
    lo = float(x.min()) - 1.0
    hi = float(x.max())
    for _ in range(50):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        if np.clip(x - mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum() > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    y = np.clip(x - 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    d = target - float(y.sum())
    if abs(d) > 1e-14:
        idx = int(np.argmin(y)) if d > 0 else int(np.argmax(y))
        if 0.0 <= y[idx] + d <= 1.0:
            y[idx] += d
        else:
            y += d / float(n)
            y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)
    return y

def interp_parent(par, n, n0):
    if n == n0:
        return par.copy()
    return np.interp(np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n), np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n0), par)

def to_binary(h, n):
    idx = np.argsort(h)[::-1]
    b = np.zeros(n)
    b[idx[:n // 2]] = 1.0
    return b

def signed_corr_fft(h, n, N=None):
    N = _fft_len(n) if N is None else int(N)
    H = np.fft.rfft(h, N)
    B = np.fft.rfft(1.0 - h, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(H * np.conj(B), N)
    pos = c[:n].copy()
    neg = np.empty(n)
    neg[0] = pos[0]
    if n > 1:
        neg[1:] = c[N - 1:N - n:-1]
    return pos, neg

def corr_from_pos_neg(pos, neg, n):
    c = np.empty(2 * n - 1)
    base = n - 1
    c[base:] = pos
    if n > 1:
        c[:base] = neg[1:][::-1]
    return c

def calc_soft(x, beta, n, N):
    h = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    pos, neg = signed_corr_fft(h, n, N)
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    vp = pos * dx
    vmax = float(vp.max())
    if n > 1 and float(neg[1:].max()) > vmax:
        vmax = float(neg[1:].max())
    ep = np.exp(np.clip(beta * (vp - vmax), -700.0, 0.0))
    en = np.zeros(n)
    if n > 1:
        en[1:] = np.exp(np.clip(beta * (neg[1:] * dx - vmax), -700.0, 0.0))
    tot = float(ep.sum() + en.sum())
    if tot <= 0.0:
        tot = 1.0
    return vmax + float(np.log(tot)) / beta, ep / tot, en / tot

def grad_from_weights(x, qpos, qneg, n, N):
    h = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    L = np.cumsum(qpos)[::-1].copy()
    L += np.cumsum(qneg)
    K = np.zeros(N)
    K[0] = 2.0 * qpos[0]
    if n > 1:
        cf = qpos[1:] + qneg[1:]
        K[1:n] = cf
        K[N - 1:N - n:-1] = cf
    C = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(h, N) * np.conj(np.fft.rfft(K, N)), N)
    T = np.empty(n)
    T[0] = C[0]
    if n > 1:
        T[1:] = C[N - 1:N - n:-1]
    return dx * (L - T)

def opt_fun(x):
    beta = float(_G['beta'])
    n = int(_G['n'])
    N = int(_G['N'])
    pen = float(_G['pen'])
    F, _, _ = calc_soft(x, beta, n, N)
    s = float(np.asarray(x).sum() - _G['target'])
    return F + pen * s * s

def opt_jac(x):
    beta = float(_G['beta'])
    n = int(_G['n'])
    N = int(_G['N'])
    pen = float(_G['pen'])
    _, qp, qn = calc_soft(x, beta, n, N)
    g = grad_from_weights(x, qp, qn, n, N)
    if pen != 0.0:
        s = float(np.asarray(x).sum() - _G['target'])
        g += 2.0 * pen * s
    return g

def cons_fun(x):
    return float(np.asarray(x).sum() - _G['target'])

def cons_jac(x):
    return np.ones(np.asarray(x).shape, dtype=float)

def slsqp_refine(h0, beta, n, N, maxiter):
    _G['beta'] = float(beta)
    _G['n'] = int(n)
    _G['N'] = int(N)
    _G['target'] = 0.5 * float(n)
    _G['pen'] = 0.0
    x0 = project_feasible(h0, n)
    bnd = [(0.0, 1.0)] * int(n)
    cons = [{'type': 'eq', 'fun': cons_fun, 'jac': cons_jac}]
    try:
        res = minimize(opt_fun, x0, method='SLSQP', jac=opt_jac, bounds=bnd,
                       constraints=cons, options={'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'ftol': 1e-12})
        x = res.x
    except Exception:
        x = x0
    return project_feasible(x, n)

def lbfgsb_refine(h0, beta, pen, n, N, maxiter):
    _G['beta'] = float(beta)
    _G['n'] = int(n)
    _G['N'] = int(N)
    _G['target'] = 0.5 * float(n)
    _G['pen'] = float(pen)
    x0 = project_feasible(h0, n)
    bnd = [(0.0, 1.0)] * int(n)
    try:
        res = minimize(opt_fun, x0, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=opt_jac, bounds=bnd,
                       options={'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'ftol': 1e-12,
                                'maxfun': int(max(100, maxiter * 12)), 'maxls': 30})
        x = res.x
    except Exception:
        x = x0
    return project_feasible(x, n)

def hard_c5_fft(h, n):
    pos, neg = signed_corr_fft(h, n, _fft_len(n))
    return float(max(float(pos.max()), float(neg.max())) * 2.0 / float(n))

def hard_c5_np(h, n):
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')) * 2.0 / float(n))

def swap_delta(h, i, j, n):
    base = n - 1
    d = np.zeros(2 * n - 1)
    R = n - 1 - i
    L = i
    if R:
        d[base + 1:base + R + 1] += 1.0 - h[i + 1:]
        d[base - R:base][::-1] -= h[i + 1:]
    if L:
        d[base - L:base][::-1] += 1.0 - h[i - 1::-1]
        d[base + 1:base + L + 1] -= h[i - 1::-1]
    R = n - 1 - j
    L = j
    if R:
        d[base + 1:base + R + 1] -= 1.0 - h[j + 1:]
        d[base - R:base][::-1] += h[j + 1:]
    if L:
        d[base - L:base][::-1] -= 1.0 - h[j - 1::-1]
        d[base + 1:base + L + 1] += h[j - 1::-1]
    d[base + j - i] += 1.0
    d[base + i - j] += 1.0
    return d

def binary_local_search(h, n, deadline, seed):
    _ = seed
    h = to_binary(h, n)
    N = _fft_len(n)
    pos, neg = signed_corr_fft(h, N := N, N)
    c = corr_from_pos_neg(pos, neg, n)
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    cur = float(c.max()) * dx
    best_c = cur
    best = h.copy()
    z = list(np.where(h == 0.0)[0])
    o = list(np.where(h == 1.0)[0])
    p = 0
    while time.time() < deadline and p < 600:
        p += 1
        bc = cur + 1e-12
        bi = -1
        bj = -1
        bd = None
        for i in z:
            for j in o:
                d = swap_delta(h, i, j, n)
                v = float((c + d).max()) * dx
                if v < bc:
                    bc = v
                    bi = i
                    bj = j
                    bd = d
        if bi < 0:
            break
        c = c + bd
        h[bi] = 1.0
        h[bj] = 0.0
        o.remove(bj)
        z.remove(bi)
        o.append(bi)
        z.append(bj)
        cur = bc
        if cur < best_c:
            best_c = cur
            best = h.copy()
        if p % 20 == 0:
            pos, neg = signed_corr_fft(h, n, N)
            c = corr_from_pos_neg(pos, neg, n)
            cur = float(c.max()) * dx
            if cur < best_c:
                best_c = cur
                best = h.copy()
    best_c = hard_c5_np(best, n)
    return best, best_c

def make_resolutions(n0):
    s = set()
    for f in (0.15, 0.2, 0.25, 0.3, 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 0.75, 0.9, 1.0, 1.25, 1.5):
        m = 2 * int(round(int(n0) * f / 2.0))
        if 64 <= m <= 640:
            s.add(m)
    for m in (64, 80, 96, 128, 160, 224, 256, 320, 384, 448, 512):
        s.add(m)
    s.add(2 * int(round(max(64, int(n0)) / 2.0)))
    return sorted(x for x in s if x % 2 == 0 and 64 <= x <= 640)

def optimize_resolution(h0, n, deadline, seed):
    n = int(n)
    h = project_feasible(h0, n)
    N = _fft_len(n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = hard_c5_np(best_h, n)
    now = time.time()
    rem = max(0.0, deadline - now)
    if rem < 10.0:
        return best_h, best_c, n
    rng = np.random.RandomState(int(seed) * 100003 + n)

    if n <= 200:
        tb = now + min(0.25 * rem, 150.0 if n <= 128 else 70.0)
        hb, cb = binary_local_search(h, n, tb, seed)
        if cb < best_c:
            best_c = cb
            best_h = hb.copy()
        if n <= 128 and time.time() < tb - 20.0:
            hr = rng.rand(n)
            hb, cb = binary_local_search(to_binary(hr, n), n, tb, seed + 1)
            if cb < best_c:
                best_c = cb
                best_h = hb.copy()

    rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.time())
    if rem > 20.0:
        t_lb = time.time() + 0.35 * rem
        if n <= 128:
            tasks = ((10.0, 10.0, 800), (50.0, 50.0, 600), (200.0, 500.0, 500))
        elif n <= 256:
            tasks = ((20.0, 20.0, 500), (100.0, 100.0, 400), (500.0, 1000.0, 300))
        else:
            tasks = ((50.0, 100.0, 300), (200.0, 1000.0, 250))
        for beta, pen, mi in tasks:
            if time.time() > t_lb - 1.0:
                break
            h2 = lbfgsb_refine(best_h, beta, pen, n, N, mi)
            c2 = hard_c5_np(h2, n)
            if c2 < best_c:
                best_c = c2
                best_h = h2.copy()
            elif c2 < best_c + 0.001:
                best_h = h2.copy()

        start = best_h.copy()
        rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.time())
        if rem > 15.0:
            t_sl = time.time() + 0.80 * rem
            if n <= 96:
                betas = (100.0, 300.0, 1000.0, 5000.0, 20000.0)
                base = 350
            elif n <= 200:
                betas = (200.0, 1000.0, 5000.0, 20000.0)
                base = 200
            elif n <= 400:
                betas = (500.0, 2000.0, 10000.0)
                base = 120
            else:
                betas = (1000.0, 5000.0)
                base = 70
            for beta in betas:
                if time.time() > t_sl - 2.0:
                    break
                mi = base
                if beta > 10000.0:
                    mi = max(60, base // 2)
                h2 = slsqp_refine(start, beta, n, N, mi)
                c2 = hard_c5_np(h2, n)
                if c2 < best_c:
                    best_c = c2
                    best_h = h2.copy()
                    start = h2.copy()
                elif c2 < best_c + 0.0005:
                    start = h2.copy()
                else:
                    start = best_h.copy()

    if time.time() < deadline - 8.0:
        bf = 30000.0 if n <= 200 else 10000.0
        mi = 220 if n <= 128 else (120 if n <= 256 else 80)
        h2 = slsqp_refine(best_h, bf, n, N, mi)
        c2 = hard_c5_np(h2, n)
        if c2 < best_c:
            best_c = c2
            best_h = h2.copy()

    if time.time() < deadline - 5.0:
        for ns in (0.002, 0.006, 0.02):
            if time.time() > deadline - 3.0:
                break
            h2 = project_feasible(best_h + ns * rng.randn(n), n)
            h2 = slsqp_refine(h2, 10000.0 if n > 200 else 30000.0, n, N,
                              90 if n > 200 else 180)
            c2 = hard_c5_np(h2, n)
            if c2 < best_c:
                best_c = c2
                best_h = h2.copy()

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, n)
    best_c = hard_c5_np(best_h, n)
    return best_h, best_c, n

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + max(5.0, float(budget_s) - 25.0)
    try:
        par = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).reshape(-1)
        n0 = len(par)
    except Exception:
        n0 = 400
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n0)
        par = 0.5 + 0.25 * np.cos(np.pi * x)
    par = project_feasible(par, n0)

    best_h = par.copy()
    best_c = hard_c5_np(best_h, n0)
    best_n = n0

    resol = make_resolutions(n0)
    cand_c = []
    cand_h = []
    for n in resol:
        if n <= 0 or n % 2:
            continue
        h = interp_parent(par, n, n0) if n != n0 else par.copy()
        h = project_feasible(h, n)
        cand_c.append(hard_c5_fft(h, n))
        cand_h.append(h)

    order = sorted(range(len(cand_c)), key=cand_c.__getitem__)
    weights = (30, 12, 6, 3, 2, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1)
    rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.time())

    for rank, ih in enumerate(order):
        if time.time() > deadline - 25.0:
            break
        if rank >= len(weights):
            break
        avail = [weights[k] if k < len(weights) else 1 for k in range(rank, len(order))]
        wsum = float(sum(avail))
        if wsum <= 0.0:
            break
        slice_time = rem * (float(avail[0]) / wsum)
        if slice_time < 10.0:
            slice_time = 0.5 * rem if rank == 0 else 0.2 * rem
        slice_time = min(slice_time, max(0.0, deadline - time.time() - 10.0))
        if slice_time <= 5.0:
            break
        h2, c2, n2 = optimize_resolution(cand_h[ih], int(2 * int(round(n2 / 2.0))),
                                         time.time() + slice_time, int(seed) + rank * 19)
        if c2 < best_c:
            best_c = c2
            best_h = h2.copy()
            best_n = int(n2)
        rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.time())

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, best_n)
    final_c = hard_c5_np(best_h, best_n)
    return best_h, final_c, int(best_n)
```
